# Library web transforms — audit

Every place the PUBLIC WEBSITE deliberately differs from the APP.
Generated by scripts/build-library-web.mjs. Do not edit.

Entries published: 92 of 97
Transforms applied: 1300

## ⚠️ Standing note — the unit rules are an ENUMERATED ALLOWLIST

`QUANTITY_PATTERNS` and `DOSE_PATTERN` in library-web-transforms.mjs list the
units they act on **by name**. They are not a general rule about numbers, and
a unit that is not listed is simply not seen.

Both misses so far were of exactly this kind, and neither was a logic error:

- **IU and `units`** were absent from the strip list, so 23 entries were about
  to publish `6,500 IU`, `50 units`, `1000 IU/mL`.
- **`µg/L`** was absent from the KEEP side, so a measured blood level was
  stripped as if it were a dose: `peak of 39.6 ± 5.4 µg/L` → `peak of 39.6 ±/L`.

**So when a new unit appears in the library, check it in BOTH directions:**

1. **strip-as-quantity** — is it ever a quantity of substance given to a person
   (a dose, or a vial/tablet strength)? If yes it must be in `QUANTITY_PATTERNS`,
   or it publishes to an ungated page.
2. **keep-as-measurement** — is it ever a measured value, a threshold or a rate
   (a blood level, an eGFR, a sodium cut-off)? If yes it must be EXCLUDED, or a
   result gets deleted and the sentence around it corrupted.

A unit can be both depending on context — `/mL` is a vial strength, `/L` is a
plasma level — so the answer is a lookahead, not a list membership. Run
`--audit` across all 97 after any change: the RESULT-STRIPPED diff is the only
check that catches direction 2, because a deleted result looks like clean prose.

**`five-amino-1mq.about[1]`**
- before: "Mechanism. **`foxo4-dri.disclosures[1]`**
- before: "⚠️ FOXO4-DRI is a senolytic peptide — it selectively kills senescent cells. Unlike the metabolic modulators, GHS-axis peptides, GLP-1 RA family, and other Vialwise library entries that produce reversible pharmacological effects, FOXO4-DRI's mechanism is cytotoxic apoptosis of senescent cells. **`hcg.about[0]`**
- before: "HCG (human chorionic gonadotropin) is a 244-amino-acid heterodimeric glycoprotein hormone, NOT a small peptide. The molecule consists of two non-covalently linked subunits: an α subunit (92 amino acids — shared with the pituitary glycoprotein hormones LH, FSH, and TSH) and a β subunit (145 amino acids — HCG-specific, with substantial structural homology to the LH β subunit). HCG is produced natura"
- after:  "HCG (human chorionic gonadotropin) is a 244-amino-acid heterodimeric glycoprotein hormone, not a small peptide. The molecule consists of two non-covalently linked subunits: an α subunit (92 amino acids — shared with the pituitary glycoprotein hormones LH, FSH, and TSH) and a β subunit (145 amino acids — HCG-specific, with substantial structural homology to the LH β subunit). HCG is produced natura"

**`hcg.about[5]`**
- before: "HCG is NOT a DEA controlled substance. Unlike testosterone (Schedule III under the Anabolic Steroid Control Act), HCG is not scheduled under federal law. It is prescription-only but does not carry the controlled-substance regulatory framework. The off-label use of HCG for TRT-adjunct testicular preservation, fertility recovery, and PCT is widely practiced under prescriber supervision in the US."
- after:  "HCG is not a DEA controlled substance. Unlike testosterone (Schedule III under the Anabolic Steroid Control Act), HCG is not scheduled under federal law. It is prescription-only but does not carry the controlled-substance regulatory framework. The off-label use of HCG for TRT-adjunct testicular preservation, fertility recovery, and PCT is widely practiced under prescriber supervision in the US."

**`hcg.stacks.combinations[3].rationale`**
- before: "sometimes added to the HCG/testosterone stack to manage estradiol elevation. HCG itself can drive estradiol elevation (because it stimulates testosterone production, and testosterone aromatizes to estradiol); some clinicians add anastrozole to manage this. The Bhasin 2018 testosterone guidelines recommend AGAINST routine AI use; this remains a case-by-case decision. See [testosterone.md](./testost"
- after:  "sometimes added to the HCG/testosterone stack to manage estradiol elevation. - after:  "Regulatory note (US): HCG is not a DEA-scheduled controlled substance but IS a prescription drug — possession outside an authorized prescriber relationship is a regulatory issue but not a controlled-substance criminal offense. **`hgh.about[7]`**
- before: "- Anti-aging / \"men's health\" clinic use — driven by the Rudman 1990 NEJM trial.[³] The trial reported lean-mass gain (+4.7 kg) and fat-mass loss (-3.5 kg) in 12 elderly men over 6 months three times weekly, but also documented carpal tunnel syndrome, gynecomastia, and glucose-tolerance impairment. Subsequent literature has not validated long-term benefit and has consistently flagged side effects."
- after:  "- Anti-aging / \"men's health\" clinic use — driven by the Rudman 1990 NEJM trial.[³] The trial reported lean-mass gain (+4.7 kg) and fat-mass loss (-3.5 kg) in 12 elderly men over 6 months three times weekly, but also documented carpal tunnel syndrome, gynecomastia, and glucose-tolerance impairment. Subsequent literature has not validated long-term benefit and has consistently flagged side effects."

**`igf-1-lr3.beginner.summary`**
- before: "IGF-1 LR3 is a modified, longer-lasting version of insulin-like growth factor-1 (IGF-1), the hormone that drives tissue and muscle growth, and it's pursued mainly for muscle growth. It's not FDA-approved and was never submitted for approval, so it's sold only"

**`melanotan-ii.beginner.summary`**
- before: "Melanotan II (MT-II) is a synthetic peptide known as 'the tanning peptide': it mimics the natural hormone alpha-MSH and darkens the skin by boosting its pigment, eumelanin. It is illegal to market, sell"

**`melanotan-ii.quickReference[5].value`**

**`melanotan-ii.about[1]`**
- before: "Mechanism. MT-II is a broad-spectrum melanocortin receptor agonist that activates four of the five melanocortin receptor subtypes:\n- MC1R (skin/hair) — drives melanogenesis (the tanning effect)\n- MC3R and MC4R (CNS) — drive sexual function effects (spontaneous erections, libido elevation), appetite suppression, and energy expenditure\n- MC5R (exocrine glands) — minor role in sebum production and ot"
- after:  "Mechanism. Mt-II is a broad-spectrum melanocortin receptor agonist that activates four of the five melanocortin receptor subtypes:\n- MC1R (skin/hair) — drives melanogenesis (the tanning effect)\n- MC3R and MC4R (CNS) — drive sexual function effects (spontaneous erections, libido elevation), appetite suppression, and energy expenditure\n- MC5R (exocrine glands) — minor role in sebum production and ot"

**`melanotan-ii.about[2]`**
- before: "This broad-spectrum receptor profile distinguishes MT-II from PT-141/bremelanotide (which has the same receptor profile but with different selectivity) and from later-generation selective MC4R agonists like setmelanotide (Imcivree, FDA-approved 2020 for genetic obesity)."
- after:  "This broad-spectrum receptor profile distinguishes mt-II from PT-141/bremelanotide (which has the same receptor profile but with different selectivity) and from later-generation selective MC4R agonists like setmelanotide (Imcivree, FDA-approved 2020 for genetic obesity)."

**`mk-677.stacks.contraindicatedCombinations[0]`**
- before: "Concurrent Ipamorelin or other GHRPs — REDUNDANT at the receptor level. MK-677 and Ipamorelin both target GHS-R1a; combining them does not produce additional GH release because the receptor is already saturated by either compound alone at therapeutic doses. Stacking is mechanistically incoherent at the GH-secretagogue level."
- after:  "Concurrent Ipamorelin or other GHRPs — redundant at the receptor level. MK-677 and Ipamorelin both target GHS-R1a; combining them does not produce additional GH release because the receptor is already saturated by either compound alone at therapeutic doses. **`exemestane.about[3]`**
- before: "Mechanism — comparison to nonsteroidal AIs. All three FDA-approved third-generation AIs act on the aromatase enzyme but exemestane's irreversible mechanism is the central pharmacological distinction. Nonsteroidal AIs (anastrozole, letrozole) bind the heme iron of the active site reversibly — pharmacological effect tracks plasma drug concentration; aromatase activity recovers as the drug clears. Ex"
- after:  "Mechanism — comparison to nonsteroidal AIs. **`linaclotide.about[5]`**

- after:  "Dose-related diarrhea. The bloating trial reports it cleanly: diarrhea in 6%, 17%, and 2% on placebo.[²] Discontinuations due to adverse events were 5% and 9% versus 6% on placebo.[²] The dose-response is exactly what the mechanism predicts."

- after:  "Use the Vialwise calculator to confirm exact insulin units for your target dose — for a first-time researcher, that is almost always weekly (the standard starting dose for both Mounjaro and Zepbound). Verify the math twice on the first draw."

**`tirzepatide.firstTimeWalkthrough[4]`**
- before: "Draw into a U-100 insulin syringe. Tap to dislodge bubbles, expel until the meniscus aligns with the calculated unit mark. At lower starting doses (5–10 units), even a one-unit error is a meaningful percentage of the dose — slow down at this step."
- after:  "Draw into a U-100 insulin syringe. Tap to dislodge bubbles, expel until the meniscus aligns with the calculated unit mark. At lower starting doses, even a one-unit error is a meaningful percentage of the dose — slow down at this step."

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: an instruction to the writer about how to phrase the claim, not the claim*

- before: "It is FORMULATION-SPECIFIC, not molecule-wide, and must never be stated either way without naming the formulation."
- after:  "It applies to some formulations and not others."

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: tells the reader about this library's other entries, not about desmopressin*

- before: "WADA status — THIS ONE IS DIFFERENT FROM ALMOST EVERY OTHER ENTRY IN THIS LIBRARY. "
- after:  "WADA status. "

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: describes what the entry does rather than stating a fact*

- before: "This entry states the classification and does not speculate about WADA's reasoning for it. "
- after:  ""

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: a note to the author about phrasing; the preceding sentences already give the reader the matrix*

- before: "A blanket \"desmopressin carries a boxed warning\" is wrong for the marketed sprays and tablets; a blanket \"it does not\" is wrong for the injection."
- after:  "The injection carries it; the marketed nasal sprays and oral tablets do not."

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: a SECOND, differently-worded copy of the same author instruction, in an About paragraph — 'marketed nasal sprays' and "it doesn't" rather than the status line's 'marketed sprays' and "it does not". Found by the mechanical check, not by eye, which is the argument for having one*

- before: ", so the formulation must be named either way. A blanket \"desmopressin carries a boxed warning\" is wrong for the marketed nasal sprays and tablets; a blanket \"it doesn't\" is wrong for the injection."
- after:  ". The injection carries it, as did the two discontinued products; the marketed nasal sprays and oral tablets do not."

**`desmopressin`**
*dose: sex-specific µg figures replaced with the qualitative finding, which is the part that matters*

- before: "sex-specific minimum effective dosing (25 µg in women, 50 µg in men, ODT formulation)"
- after:  "sex-specific minimum effective dosing, lower in women than in men, using the orally disintegrating form"

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: an author's note to self inside a table cell*

- before: "| Currently marketed — this is the live one |"
- after:  "| Currently marketed |"

**`desmopressin`**
*editorial: a within-entry cross-reference written for the author*

- before: " ⚠️ The injection is the formulation carrying the boxed warning (see About)."
- after:  " The injection is the formulation that carries the boxed warning."

**`retatrutide`**
*dose: the sentence MAPS three dose levels onto three results, so removing the doses breaks the correspondence and leaves 'Over 40 weeks at, participants'. 'three ascending dose levels' keeps the reader able to line the figures up*

- before: "Over 40 weeks at 4 / 9 / 12mg, participants"
- after:  "Over 40 weeks at three ascending dose levels, participants"

a name or acronym than shouted emphasis (see calmTone). Frequency is the
